# SWEEP #10 — TERMINAL 30 · THE MONEY AND COMPLIANCE LIBRARIES · P199001–P200000

> **This is NOT `T30.md`** (terminal 30 of sweep #6) and not `T24.md` / `T34-S9.md`, which held most
> of this ground in sweeps #6 and #9. Terminal numbers are per-sweep.

**Territory (20 files):** `lib/tax.ts` · `lib/taxFiling.ts` · `lib/paySplit.ts` · `lib/payments.ts` ·
`lib/discountCap.ts` · `lib/clash.ts` · `lib/clashCompare.ts` · `lib/idempotency.ts` ·
`lib/idempotencyRule.ts` · `lib/dbRefusal.ts` · `lib/readGuard.ts` · `lib/money.ts` · `lib/money.mjs` ·
`lib/money.test.mjs` · `tests/money.test.mjs` · `tests/order-totals.e2e.mjs` ·
`docs/COMPLIANCE-GUARDRAILS.md` · `docs/SAAS-EFFICIENCY-PLAYBOOK.md` · `docs/CANCEL-AND-LOSS-SPEC.md` ·
`docs/BUSINESS-LOGIC-AUDIT.md`.

**Branch:** `sweep10/t30-money-and-compliance` · **worktree:** `~/Documents/Projects/wt-s10-t30` · **port:** 4430

**ID block:** `P199001`–`P200000`, pre-allocated. Used: `P199001`–`P199670` (the static/unit/database
runner, per-file sub-ranges), `P199671`–`P199702` (driven), `P199721`–`P199742` (screenshots read by
eye), `P199751`–`P199820` (per-route / per-call-site). `INDEX.md`'s *Next free ID* line was never read
and never edited.

**Result key:** ✅ pass · ❌ fail · ⏭ unanswered, with a written reason.

## How to re-run every row here

```sh
node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings scripts/sweep/t30s10/run.mjs          # P199001–P199670, P199751+ (+ 31 re-run rows)
node scripts/sweep/t30s10/live.mjs  --base http://localhost:<port>                  # P199671–P199702 (+ P20018, P07456)
node scripts/sweep/t30s10/shots.mjs --base http://localhost:<port>                  # P199721–P199737 (then READ the PNGs)
```

`run.mjs` writes nothing (database checks are SELECTs on the dev project only; `lib/paySplit.ts` runs
against an in-memory recording stand-in). `live.mjs` signs in once per role and sends only writes the
app refuses; the one setting it could move is read first and restored in a `finally` and on a signal.

## What was re-run BEFORE any of these were written

**285 existing rows** name a file in this territory: 235 by subject across 14 ledger files (172 of
them in sweep #6's `T24.md`) plus all 50 of sweep #9's `T34-S9.md`, which was this same territory.
All 285 were re-run on 2026-10-09 and their results updated **in place**, each with a
`s10·T30 2026-10-09` note naming the command. **One regression: P14078** — ✅ when written ("the
cancel spec names no migration, so it cannot be stale"), ❌ today: the spec had since gained a §4b that
called the work "Migration 337"; it is 340. Fixed as item 1. P24563's old ⏭ is answered (28 files now
use `lib/readGuard`). P14525 and P44206 stay ⏭ for the reasons written on them.

Guards run for the re-run: `verify-t24-money-rules --db` (254/254), `verify-t24b-money-safety`
(432/432), `verify-t24b-live` on :4430 (66/66), `scripts/sweep/t34/run.mjs` (87: 86 ✅ 1 ⏭),
`verify-admin-money` on :4430, `verify:t14` (836 ✅), `test:money`, `test:units`, `test:totals`.

the code and the live database), and 54 of them DRIVE the real app or read a real screen.

## Problems found and fixed (one commit each, every one with a guard)

1. **(regression, P14078)** the cancel spec named migration 337 for work in 340 — guard `scripts/verify-money-pointers.mjs` (new).
2. `lib/tax.ts` pointed at migrations 269/271 for functions in 270/272 — same guard, check 1.
3. the compliance doc's purge paragraph was stale (a count of six, "reads 342 only") — check 4 + `verify:t24-money-rules`.
4. Pay in parts read a failed database read as an answer ("already paid", a guessed 5%) — `verify:split-payment` block 9.
5. two devices settling one bill at once both succeeded → the drawer asked for double — `verify:split-payment` block 9, `verify:t24-money-rules`, `verify:t24b`.
6. Pay in parts showed the waiter the database's own sentence — `verify:split-payment`, `verify:plain-refusals` (now catches a chained `x.error.message`).
7. Show / Reset password left the plaintext password in the stored-replies table — `verify:order-retry` block 1b (+ 37 dev rows cleared).
8. the playbook located a loop by a stale line number — `verify-money-pointers` check 5.
9. menu categories and filters had no first-save-wins gate (looked up by an `id` column they do not have) — `verify:t24-money-rules` (static + `--db`).
10. the playbook named two analytics indexes that had been dropped — `verify-money-pointers` check 6.

## ROUND 2 (owner, 2026-10-09: *"start a test of … 500 to 1000 … make sure there shouldn't be any error left … check every single bit"*)

**Ids:** `P168001`–`P168999` — taken from the top of the planning gap below sweep #10's blocks (T17
works upward from `P161001`; nothing used `P1680xx`), because this terminal's own block had 206 ids
left. Recorded in this terminal's INDEX row. Re-checked with `verify:ledger-index`: no collision.

**What was re-planned.** Round 1 checked claims. Round 2 makes "every bit" MEASURABLE:

1. `scripts/t30-harness/` loads the **real** 13 code files with the database replaced by an
   in-memory stand-in (every fetch refused), and runs **603 checks** (15 suites, one per file plus
   proofs and equivalences) — `npm run verify:t30-money`.
2. **Coverage** — Node's built-in coverage: **every line and every branch of all 13 files ran**,
   except 10 branches that are each **proven unreachable** by their own check — `npm run verify:t30-coverage`.
3. **Mutation testing** — **384 deliberate breaks** (=== ↔ !==, && ↔ ||, >= ↔ >, true ↔ false, ±1, a
   dropped `!`, `?? 0` → 1): **362 caught by a check, 22 proven equivalent** (each run side by side with
   the real code over thousands of inputs, or fuzzed with 3,000 odd inputs), **0 unexplained** —
   `npm run verify:t30-mutants`.
4. All of round 1 re-run on the changed code: 480 static/database + 34 driven on :4430 + 17 screens.

**Fixed in round 2:** items 11–14 (the owner said "do all within your boundaries"): a stored reply is
repeated only to whoever made it · a list of objects is compared by content · LFH04 registered and
LFH03's two meanings told apart · three pieces of unused money code removed. **Found by round 2:**
item 22 (the short rupee label printed "₹1000" for ₹999.50–₹999.99) and item 23 (Pay in parts'
two-paise tolerance answered differently on different bills). Two crash shapes only a broken copy
could reach (`fields` / `where` given as null) are now pinned by checks.

## ROUND 3 (owner, 2026-10-09: *"do all … start a test of … 500 to 1000 … check every single bit … replan whole test … I want zero"*)

**Ids:** `P167001`–`P167999`, claimed on `main` (PR #1465) BEFORE a row was written; `P168941`–`P168968`
(the round-3 items' own suite) sit in this terminal's round-2 block. **507 new rows, 503 ✅ · 4 ❌.**
Every earlier row of this ledger that a runner executes was re-run on the round-3 code: **1087 rows refreshed**,
all green — four of them RE-STATED because their subject moved on purpose (P168421–P168424: item 25 quotes
text; P168217: item 27 makes a failed read throw; P199566–P199567: items 16–17 ticked the playbook;
P199782: T10's item 4 measures the per-line cap against the whole bill).

**What was re-planned.** Rounds 1–2 proved each line with chosen examples. Round 3 asks four new questions:
1. **Does each RULE hold for every input?** — `scripts/t30-harness/u-props.mjs`: each money rule run against
   thousands of seeded random inputs (the bill identity, screen = printed bill = Pay in parts, the GST
   filing's sums, the comparison rule, secrets, refusals, reads, chart money; every refusal code one by one).
2. **Do the app and the database agree?** — `scripts/sweep/t30s10/parity.mjs`, read-only SQL on dev: the
   rate and each dish's behaviour for all 288 restaurants; carts through `lfh_split_items_tax` vs `splitBill`
   per set-up and per named restaurant; the rounding under both on every amount to ₹10,000; each
   restaurant's saved orders against eight rules; **5,695 saved orders re-computed from their own dishes —
   all four figures equal**; every table, column, filter and function the 14 files use, recorded from a full
   harness run and checked against the catalog, with an index under every query.
3. **Every export** has a job and a check (`u-exports.mjs`); **every line, branch and break** of the 14 files
   (coverage + a FULL mutation run, rows below).
4. **Driven for real** on :4430 (`live.mjs`): the allergy line on the manager AND tablet routes; a tap sent
   twice runs once; the owner's GST report for all ten periods rebuilt with the real `buildFiling`.

**Fixed in round 3** (one commit each, each with checks that fail on the old code): items 15–18 and 21 (the
owner said "do all five") and four the test itself found — **24** (a failed allergy write could not heal on
retry; dish 501 was skipped), **25** (two different values could compare equal in "first save wins"),
**26** (a GST split could print a negative tax line), **27** (undoing a bill paid in parts could leave its
money counted as collected; the tablet did the two steps in the risky order).

**The 4 ❌ are one open decision, not four faults:** the app rounds half a paisa with floating point, the
database exactly (P167532–P167535). The fix is one exact rounding rule shared by `lib/tax.ts` AND
`public/panels/billdoc.js` — outside this terminal's boundary, so the owner decides. On the dev data no
saved order is off by it (P167623–P167633).

## ROUND 4 (owner, 2026-10-10: *"do all the things you have listed and then start a test … 500 to 1000 … replan whole test … I want zero error"*)

**Ids:** `P166001`–`P166999`, claimed on `main` (PR #1470) BEFORE a row was written; plus the round-4 rows
appended to suites that live in this terminal's earlier blocks (u-props, u-equivalence, parity P167655–P167656).
**516 new rows, 516 ✅ · 0 ❌.** Every earlier row a runner executes was re-run on the
round-4 code: **1557 rows refreshed, all green.** Re-stated because their subject moved on purpose (item 10
made rounding exact): u-props P167009 / P167017 / P167022 / P167116, verify-t24b's float helper, parity's app side
of P167532–P167535 (now GREEN — the app and the database agree); P167567 (item 12 emptied the platform names, so
the rule became absolute); P166455 (mig 419 adds back one ratings policy).

**Fixed in round 4** (one commit each; each with checks red on the old code): items **10** (one exact rounding to the
paisa everywhere — the owner's yes to the shared bill file), **11** (the database refuses a made-up payment method,
mig 417), **12–15** (the film seeder and its 2,730 rows, six demo stamps, a test rig's ₹1,449 of parts, four idle
tables), **16** (a dish page learns which review is mine, never every device id, mig 418) — and three the test itself
found: **28** (the waiter tablet charged a composition-scheme restaurant 5%), **29** (a REGRESSION of item 16: the
guest menu's ratings vanished, because the ratings view runs with the guest's rights — mig 419, caught by reading a
screenshot), and taxFiling's no-import rule restored (it had been broken by item 10's first shape).

**What was re-planned.** (1) The four copies of the bill rule — lib/tax.ts, the manager cart, the waiter tablet, the
printed bill — lifted out of the panels and run against each other for every one of the 72 tax set-ups a restaurant can
choose; (2) whole bills: the printed bill against the database's own sums at six rates, the trigger-written tax of an
order with an untaxed line, the GST report against the paper's CGST/SGST split; (3) the new database rules exercised:
nine made-up payment methods refused and seven accepted (each inside a block that always rolls back, the order proven
unchanged), every reviewed dish at both restaurants through lfh_dish_reviews, the ratings view asked AS the guest role;
(4) the data repairs holding; (5) everything earlier re-run, the changed files re-mutated, the pages driven and read.
